\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

finish\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch -\/ clean up after a task-\/switch @prev\+: the thread we just switched away from.

finish\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch must be called after the context switch, paired with a prepare\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch call before the context switch. finish\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch will reconcile locking set up by prepare\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch, and do any other architecture-\/specific cleanup actions.

Note that we may have delayed dropping an mm in \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_aec98ae4c131db50476fbe13b498c2c3d}{context\+\_\+switch()}}. If so, we finish that here outside of the runqueue lock. (Doing it with the lock held can cause deadlocks; see \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a7366dbeb1fb6baba9c376a2310e2d966}{schedule()}} for details.)

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} context switch have flipped the stack from under us and restored the local variables which were saved when this task called \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a7366dbeb1fb6baba9c376a2310e2d966}{schedule()}} in the past. prev == current is still correct but we need to recalculate this\+\_\+rq because prev may have moved to another C\+PU. {\footnotesize\ttfamily static void prepare\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structrq}{rq}} $\ast$}]{rq,  }\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{prev,  }\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{next }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

prepare\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch -\/ prepare to switch tasks @rq\+: the runqueue preparing to switch @prev\+: the current task that is being switched out @next\+: the task we are going to switch to.

This is called with the rq lock held and interrupts off. It must be paired with a subsequent finish\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch after the context switch.

prepare\+\_\+task\+\_\+switch sets up locking and calls architecture specific hooks. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

sched\+\_\+setscheduler\+\_\+nocheck -\/ change the scheduling policy and/or RT priority of a thread from kernelspace. {\ttfamily }\+: the task in question. @policy\+: new policy. 
\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em } & structure containing the new RT priority.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
Just like sched\+\_\+setscheduler, only don\textquotesingle{}t bother checking if the current context has permission. For example, this is needed in \mbox{\hyperlink{stop__machine_8c_accd2ff0c7479d51505cabf6f8bc5e973}{stop\+\_\+machine()}}\+: we create temporary high priority worker threads, but our caller might not have that capability.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_afaa87723b8417d40fcf45b7330261ef9}{bool}} try\+\_\+invoke\+\_\+on\+\_\+locked\+\_\+down\+\_\+task (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{p,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_afaa87723b8417d40fcf45b7330261ef9}{bool}}($\ast$)(struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$\mbox{\hyperlink{decode__rs_8c_a735e168770764edbf1b02c6299bf4286}{t}}, void $\ast$arg)}]{func,  }\item[{void $\ast$}]{arg }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

try\+\_\+invoke\+\_\+on\+\_\+locked\+\_\+down\+\_\+task -\/ Invoke a function on task in fixed state {\ttfamily }\+: Process for which the function is to be invoked, can be @current. @func\+: Function to invoke. \begin{DoxyItemize}
\item \+: Argument to function.\end{DoxyItemize}
If the specified task can be quickly locked into a definite state (either sleeping or on a given runqueue), arrange to keep it in that state while invoking @func(\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item ). This function can use -\/$>$on\+\_\+rq and \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a5aa3e33ce99530bf0b727bbb49a3330c}{task\+\_\+curr()}} to work out what the state is, if required. Given that @func can be invoked with a runqueue lock held, it had better be quite lightweight.\end{DoxyItemize}
Returns\+: @false if the task slipped out from under the locks. @true if the task was locked onto a runqueue or is sleeping. However, @func can override this by returning @false. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 209
\mbox{\Hypertarget{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a2314337556104bfb2efb3446a515d0b1}\label{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a2314337556104bfb2efb3446a515d0b1}} 
\index{core.c@{core.c}!try\_to\_wake\_up@{try\_to\_wake\_up}}
\index{try\_to\_wake\_up@{try\_to\_wake\_up}!core.c@{core.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{try\_to\_wake\_up()}{try\_to\_wake\_up()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} try\+\_\+to\+\_\+wake\+\_\+up (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{p,  }\item[{unsigned \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}}}]{state,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}}}]{wake\+\_\+flags }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

try\+\_\+to\+\_\+wake\+\_\+up -\/ wake up a thread {\ttfamily }\+: the thread to be awakened @state\+: the mask of task states that can be woken @wake\+\_\+flags\+: wake modifier flags (W\+F\+\_\+$\ast$)

Conceptually does\+:

If (@state \& {\ttfamily }-\/$>$state) {\ttfamily }-\/$>$state = T\+A\+S\+K\+\_\+\+R\+U\+N\+N\+I\+NG.

If the task was not queued/runnable, also place it back on a runqueue.

This function is atomic against \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a7366dbeb1fb6baba9c376a2310e2d966}{schedule()}} which would dequeue the task.

It issues a full memory barrier before accessing {\ttfamily }-\/$>$state, see the comment with set\+\_\+current\+\_\+state().

Uses p-\/$>$pi\+\_\+lock to serialize against concurrent wake-\/ups.

Relies on p-\/$>$pi\+\_\+lock stabilizing\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item p-\/$>$\mbox{\hyperlink{structsched__class}{sched\+\_\+class}}
\item p-\/$>$cpus\+\_\+ptr
\item p-\/$>$sched\+\_\+task\+\_\+group in order to do migration, see its use of select\+\_\+task\+\_\+rq()/set\+\_\+task\+\_\+cpu().
\end{DoxyItemize}

Tries really hard to only take one \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2sched_8h_a4ed7787b91f110f9a833b6c4cfabaa69}{task\+\_\+rq(p)}}-\/$>$lock for performance. Takes rq-\/$>$lock in\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a45989c62a6083d9fa286e68583be272b}{ttwu\+\_\+runnable()}} -- old rq, unavoidable, see comment there;
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a8e3c33dbbb81407431f23a89feb90549}{ttwu\+\_\+queue()}} -- new rq, for enqueue of the task;
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2stats_8h_ae3a546633c7a79381ecd4afbcd4502cd}{psi\+\_\+ttwu\+\_\+dequeue()}} -- much sadness \+:-\/( accounting will kill us.
\end{DoxyItemize}

As a consequence we race really badly with just about everything. See the many memory barriers and their comments for details.

Return\+: true if {\ttfamily }-\/$>$state changes (an actual wakeup was done), false otherwise. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_ad696374828c592d476846800b99340a3}{wake\+\_\+q\+\_\+add()}} -\/ queue a wakeup for \textquotesingle{}later\textquotesingle{} waking. @head\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{structwake__q__head}{wake\+\_\+q\+\_\+head}} to add @task to @task\+: the task to queue for \textquotesingle{}later\textquotesingle{} wakeup

Queue a task for later wakeup, most likely by the \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_adaefbfde5a74119541f91aa568ef5dd7}{wake\+\_\+up\+\_\+q()}} call in the same context, {\itshape H\+O\+W\+E\+V\+ER} this is not guaranteed, the wakeup can come instantly.

\mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_ad309e72f9acc7e1be2b271b0a007619c}{wake\+\_\+q\+\_\+add\+\_\+safe()}} -\/ safely queue a wakeup for \textquotesingle{}later\textquotesingle{} waking. @head\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{structwake__q__head}{wake\+\_\+q\+\_\+head}} to add @task to @task\+: the task to queue for \textquotesingle{}later\textquotesingle{} wakeup

Queue a task for later wakeup, most likely by the \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_adaefbfde5a74119541f91aa568ef5dd7}{wake\+\_\+up\+\_\+q()}} call in the same context, {\itshape H\+O\+W\+E\+V\+ER} this is not guaranteed, the wakeup can come instantly.

This function must be used as-\/if it were \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a53212be4e2644aeff9ffd4bc19f7186e}{wake\+\_\+up\+\_\+process()}}; I\+OW the task must be ready to be woken at this location.

This function is essentially a task-\/safe equivalent to \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_ad696374828c592d476846800b99340a3}{wake\+\_\+q\+\_\+add()}}. Callers that already hold reference to @task can call the \textquotesingle{}safe\textquotesingle{} version and trust wake\+\_\+q to do the right thing depending whether or not the @task is already queued for wakeup. wake\+\_\+up\+\_\+process -\/ Wake up a specific process {\ttfamily }\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} process to be woken up.

Attempt to wake up the nominated process and move it to the set of runnable processes.

Return\+: 1 if the process was woken up, 0 if it was already running.

\mbox{\Hypertarget{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}\label{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}} 
\index{core.c@{core.c}!yield@{yield}}
\index{yield@{yield}!core.c@{core.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{yield()}{yield()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2sched_2debug_8h_a4e921e4f66bcb641532ae81d2b50b237}{\+\_\+\+\_\+sched}} yield (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

yield -\/ yield the current processor to other threads.

Do not ever use this function, there\textquotesingle{}s a 99\% chance you\textquotesingle{}re doing it wrong.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} scheduler is at all times free to pick the calling task as the most eligible task to run, if removing the \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}{yield()}} call from your code breaks it, it\textquotesingle{}s already broken.

Typical broken usage is\+:

while (!event) \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}{yield()}};

where one assumes that \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}{yield()}} will let \textquotesingle{}the other\textquotesingle{} process run that will make event true. If the current task is a S\+C\+H\+E\+D\+\_\+\+F\+I\+FO task that will never happen. Never use \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}{yield()}} as a progress guarantee!!

If you want to use \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}{yield()}} to wait for something, use wait\+\_\+event(). If you want to use \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}{yield()}} to be \textquotesingle{}nice\textquotesingle{} for others, use cond\+\_\+resched(). If you still want to use \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_a565f425fa355baeb8fe6722d765332eb}{yield()}}, do not! Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 245
\mbox{\Hypertarget{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_ab222a2197eb3eecc4d50a53681c0989b}\label{kernel_2sched_2core_8c_ab222a2197eb3eecc4d50a53681c0989b}}

\index{yield\_to@{yield\_to}!core.c@{core.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{yield\_to()}{yield\_to()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2sched_2debug_8h_a4e921e4f66bcb641532ae81d2b50b237}{\+\_\+\+\_\+sched}} yield\+\_\+to (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{p,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_afaa87723b8417d40fcf45b7330261ef9}{bool}}}]{preempt }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

yield\+\_\+to -\/ yield the current processor to another thread in your thread group, or accelerate that thread toward the processor it\textquotesingle{}s on. {\ttfamily }\+: target task @preempt\+: whether task preemption is allowed or not

It\textquotesingle{}s the caller\textquotesingle{}s job to ensure that the target task struct can\textquotesingle{}t go away on us before we can do any checks.

Return\+: true ($>$0) if we indeed boosted the target task. false (0) if we failed to boost the target. -\/E\+S\+R\+CH if there\textquotesingle{}s no task to yield to. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 246
